And I used to like going to Cubs games, even when they lost, which was often enough, but that was part of it somehow.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People say, “Come on, you’ll feel better if you go,” but I can’t seem to make myself care. It’s like all those things belong to somebody else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, everybody has spells, don’t they? After my mother passed, years ago, I was low for a bit, and when I retired it took some getting used to… I used to have a whole routine, coffee in the same mug, paper folded just so before work.

I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.

It’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.

I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.

Before this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.

So I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.

Winters used to be something, you know, when the kids were small and we’d shovel the walk, make cocoa… but now I just think, what’s the point of another one?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like making cocoa or being with your family?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I suppose that’s right. I used to look forward to things—bridge night, fussing over the garden, helping with the cooking at church. And I used to like going to Cubs games, even when they lost, which was often enough, but that was part of it somehow.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People say, “Come on, you’ll feel better if you go,” but I can’t seem to make myself care. It’s like all those things belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have you had times like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt so down and lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… oh, about a year now. A year and a month, maybe. It started after my bowling partner died. Funny, we weren’t even very good bowlers—he had this way of blaming the lanes every Tuesday—but after he was gone, things just… went flat. Since then, I don’t think I’ve really come back from it.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, everybody has spells, don’t they? After my mother passed, years ago, I was low for a bit, and when I retired it took some getting used to… I used to have a whole routine, coffee in the same mug, paper folded just so before work.\n\nBut more than two weeks like this? I don’t know. I can’t really say. It all blurs a little when I try to look back.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I… I really can’t say for sure. I know I had sad stretches, like anyone does—life sort of knocks you around, and then you get up and do the shopping or mow the lawn because it needs doing.\n\nBut more than two weeks in a row, and losing interest the way you mean… I don’t know. I’d be guessing. Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. Before this last year… I just don’t know. There were bad patches, sure, but whether they were more than two weeks straight, or whether I lost interest like that… I can’t honestly say.\n\nIt’s strange, I can tell you the name of the man who ran the shoe counter at the bowling alley years ago, but this kind of thing just slips around when I try to remember it.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.\n\nIt’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose what brings me in is I’ve been having these thoughts, and they don’t let up. I keep thinking my wife and the kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I know that’s a terrible thing to say.\n\nAnd lately I’ve been telling people—or maybe just muttering it, I don’t know—that I don’t need to see another winter. Winters used to be something, you know, when the kids were small and we’d shovel the walk, make cocoa… but now I just think, what’s the point of another one?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like making cocoa or being with your family?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I suppose that’s right. I used to look forward to things—bridge night, fussing over the garden, helping with the cooking at church. And I used to like going to Cubs games, even when they lost, which was often enough, but that was part of it somehow.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People say, “Come on, you’ll feel better if you go,” but I can’t seem to make myself care. It’s like all those things belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have you had times like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt so down and lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… oh, about a year now. A year and a month, maybe. It started after my bowling partner died. Funny, we weren’t even very good bowlers—he had this way of blaming the lanes every Tuesday—but after he was gone, things just… went flat. Since then, I don’t think I’ve really come back from it.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, everybody has spells, don’t they? After my mother passed, years ago, I was low for a bit, and when I retired it took some getting used to… I used to have a whole routine, coffee in the same mug, paper folded just so before work.\n\nBut more than two weeks like this? I don’t know. I can’t really say. It all blurs a little when I try to look back.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I… I really can’t say for sure. I know I had sad stretches, like anyone does—life sort of knocks you around, and then you get up and do the shopping or mow the lawn because it needs doing.\n\nBut more than two weeks in a row, and losing interest the way you mean… I don’t know. I’d be guessing. Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. Before this last year… I just don’t know. There were bad patches, sure, but whether they were more than two weeks straight, or whether I lost interest like that… I can’t honestly say.\n\nIt’s strange, I can tell you the name of the man who ran the shoe counter at the bowling alley years ago, but this kind of thing just slips around when I try to remember it.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.\n\nIt’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was a shorter time or not as intense, can you recall if you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to help me narrow it down, doctor, and I keep circling the same answer. So… I don’t want to guess and say yes or no. I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as intense, did you ever have a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a few ways, and I’m sorry, I don’t mean to be difficult. I just can’t honestly say.\n\nThere were times before this past year when I felt kind of down or not interested in much, but whether it was more than two weeks straight… I don’t have that clear in my head. No… I can’t say for sure. That’s the honest answer.

I know there were spells where I wasn’t myself, where things felt dull, but when you say “more than two weeks in a row,” I lose it. I start trying to count backward and then I’m remembering the curtains in my old place, or how the mail used to come late on Tuesdays, and it all gets mixed up.

Winters used to be something, you know, when the kids were small and we’d shovel the walk, make cocoa… but now I just think, what’s the point of another one?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like making cocoa or being with your family?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I suppose that’s right. I used to look forward to things—bridge night, fussing over the garden, helping with the cooking at church. And I used to like going to Cubs games, even when they lost, which was often enough, but that was part of it somehow.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People say, “Come on, you’ll feel better if you go,” but I can’t seem to make myself care. It’s like all those things belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have you had times like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt so down and lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… oh, about a year now. A year and a month, maybe. It started after my bowling partner died. Funny, we weren’t even very good bowlers—he had this way of blaming the lanes every Tuesday—but after he was gone, things just… went flat. Since then, I don’t think I’ve really come back from it.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, everybody has spells, don’t they? After my mother passed, years ago, I was low for a bit, and when I retired it took some getting used to… I used to have a whole routine, coffee in the same mug, paper folded just so before work.\n\nBut more than two weeks like this? I don’t know. I can’t really say. It all blurs a little when I try to look back.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I… I really can’t say for sure. I know I had sad stretches, like anyone does—life sort of knocks you around, and then you get up and do the shopping or mow the lawn because it needs doing.\n\nBut more than two weeks in a row, and losing interest the way you mean… I don’t know. I’d be guessing. Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. Before this last year… I just don’t know. There were bad patches, sure, but whether they were more than two weeks straight, or whether I lost interest like that… I can’t honestly say.\n\nIt’s strange, I can tell you the name of the man who ran the shoe counter at the bowling alley years ago, but this kind of thing just slips around when I try to remember it.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.\n\nIt’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was a shorter time or not as intense, can you recall if you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to help me narrow it down, doctor, and I keep circling the same answer. I can remember being low before, yes, but whether it stayed that way for more than two weeks straight… I just can’t say for sure.\n\nMy memory goes off in pieces. I’ll remember the smell of the old bus station coffee, or what shoes I wore to a wedding forty years ago, but not the stretch of days well enough to answer that cleanly. So… I don’t want to guess and say yes or no. I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as intense, did you ever have a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a few ways, and I’m sorry, I don’t mean to be difficult. I just can’t honestly say.\n\nThere were times before this past year when I felt kind of down or not interested in much, but whether it was more than two weeks straight… I don’t have that clear in my head. I start trying to remember and then I’m thinking about the apartment I had back then, or the old radio in the kitchen, and I lose the thread of the days.\n\nSo the best I can tell you is: I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was shorter or not as strong, can you say for sure that you had a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t say for sure. That’s the honest answer.\n\nI know there were spells where I wasn’t myself, where things felt dull, but when you say “more than two weeks in a row,” I lose it. I start trying to count backward and then I’m remembering the curtains in my old place, or how the mail used to come late on Tuesdays, and it all gets mixed up.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you something definite when I don’t really know. I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don’t know. I can’t really say. I’d be guessing. Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. I really do. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was a shorter time or not as intense, can you recall if you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to help me narrow it down, doctor, and I keep circling the same answer. I can remember being low before, yes, but whether it stayed that way for more than two weeks straight… I just can’t say for sure.\n\nMy memory goes off in pieces. So… I don’t want to guess and say yes or no. I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as intense, did you ever have a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a few ways, and I’m sorry, I don’t mean to be difficult. That’s the honest answer.\n\nI know there were spells where I wasn’t myself, where things felt dull, but when you say “more than two weeks in a row,” I lose it. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it wasn’t as severe or long-lasting, can you recall a specific time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row—like a clear stretch of time where you weren’t yourself?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a clear stretch like that, but I can’t pull one out for sure.\n\nI remember feeling off at different times, sure… but when I try to pin it down to “more than two weeks in a row,” it gets slippery. Well… I’m still not sure about before this past year. Several days, maybe, but I can’t put my finger on it cleanly.

What I can tell you is now it’s very clear. I used to look forward to bridge, and fussing with the garden, and helping with the cooking at church. I used to enjoy going to Cubs games too—though parking was always a mess, and I remember one time we sat way up where the wind just cut through you…

But now I mostly just sit in my chair. I think about those things and I can’t make myself care enough to do them. It’s like the wanting part is gone.

Before this past year… I still can’t say for sure, no. I don’t want to make it sound like I remember a clean two-week stretch when I don’t.

Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. Before this last year… I just don’t know. There were bad patches, sure, but whether they were more than two weeks straight, or whether I lost interest like that… I can’t honestly say.\n\nIt’s strange, I can tell you the name of the man who ran the shoe counter at the bowling alley years ago, but this kind of thing just slips around when I try to remember it.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.\n\nIt’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was a shorter time or not as intense, can you recall if you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to help me narrow it down, doctor, and I keep circling the same answer. I can remember being low before, yes, but whether it stayed that way for more than two weeks straight… I just can’t say for sure.\n\nMy memory goes off in pieces. I’ll remember the smell of the old bus station coffee, or what shoes I wore to a wedding forty years ago, but not the stretch of days well enough to answer that cleanly. So… I don’t want to guess and say yes or no. I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as intense, did you ever have a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a few ways, and I’m sorry, I don’t mean to be difficult. I just can’t honestly say.\n\nThere were times before this past year when I felt kind of down or not interested in much, but whether it was more than two weeks straight… I don’t have that clear in my head. I start trying to remember and then I’m thinking about the apartment I had back then, or the old radio in the kitchen, and I lose the thread of the days.\n\nSo the best I can tell you is: I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was shorter or not as strong, can you say for sure that you had a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t say for sure. That’s the honest answer.\n\nI know there were spells where I wasn’t myself, where things felt dull, but when you say “more than two weeks in a row,” I lose it. I start trying to count backward and then I’m remembering the curtains in my old place, or how the mail used to come late on Tuesdays, and it all gets mixed up.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you something definite when I don’t really know. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it wasn’t as severe or long-lasting, can you recall a specific time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row—like a clear stretch of time where you weren’t yourself?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a clear stretch like that, but I can’t pull one out for sure.\n\nI remember feeling off at different times, sure… but when I try to pin it down to “more than two weeks in a row,” it gets slippery. I start thinking about where I was living then, and there was this little grocery store on the corner with a bell on the door—funny the things that stick—but the actual days, whether it was two weeks or just a few bad days spread out… I don’t know.\n\nSo no, I can’t recall a specific clear time before this past year. Not one I’d swear to.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was not as severe, can you recall a time when you felt down or stopped caring for a clear stretch of time—like several days in a row—where it felt like you were not yourself?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m still not sure about before this past year. Several days, maybe, but I can’t put my finger on it cleanly.\n\nWhat I can tell you is now it’s very clear. I used to look forward to bridge, and fussing with the garden, and helping with the cooking at church. I used to enjoy going to Cubs games too—though parking was always a mess, and I remember one time we sat way up where the wind just cut through you…\n\nBut now I mostly just sit in my chair. I think about those things and I can’t make myself care enough to do them. It’s like the wanting part is gone.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as strong, can you say for sure that there was a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Before this past year… I still can’t say for sure, no. I don’t want to make it sound like I remember a clean two-week stretch when I don’t.\n\nWhat I do know is how it is now. I used to look forward to bridge, and the garden, and helping with the cooking at church. And Cubs games—I used to get excited about those, even if the seats were uncomfortable and the hot dogs cost too much. Now I just sit in my chair and think about all that, and I can’t make myself care enough to do any of it. It’s like it’s all behind glass or something.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know you’re asking about before this past year, but I still can’t honestly say yes to that. More than two weeks in a row… I just don’t have a clear memory of that.

There were times I wasn’t quite right, I suppose, but then I start mixing up when things happened—who was visiting, whether it was summer or winter, silly things like whether the Cubs were doing well that year—and I can’t tell if it was really two weeks or just a few bad days here and there.

So… no, not one I can clearly recall.

Winters used to be something, you know, when the kids were small and we’d shovel the walk, make cocoa… but now I just think, what’s the point of another one?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like making cocoa or being with your family?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I suppose that’s right. I used to look forward to things—bridge night, fussing over the garden, helping with the cooking at church. And I used to like going to Cubs games, even when they lost, which was often enough, but that was part of it somehow.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People say, “Come on, you’ll feel better if you go,” but I can’t seem to make myself care. It’s like all those things belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have you had times like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt so down and lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… oh, about a year now. A year and a month, maybe. It started after my bowling partner died. Funny, we weren’t even very good bowlers—he had this way of blaming the lanes every Tuesday—but after he was gone, things just… went flat. Since then, I don’t think I’ve really come back from it.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, everybody has spells, don’t they? I don’t know. I can’t really say. It all blurs a little when I try to look back.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I… I really can’t say for sure. I know I had sad stretches, like anyone does—life sort of knocks you around, and then you get up and do the shopping or mow the lawn because it needs doing.\n\nBut more than two weeks in a row, and losing interest the way you mean… I don’t know. I’d be guessing. Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. Before this last year… I just don’t know. There were bad patches, sure, but whether they were more than two weeks straight, or whether I lost interest like that… I can’t honestly say.\n\nIt’s strange, I can tell you the name of the man who ran the shoe counter at the bowling alley years ago, but this kind of thing just slips around when I try to remember it.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.\n\nIt’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was a shorter time or not as intense, can you recall if you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to help me narrow it down, doctor, and I keep circling the same answer. I can remember being low before, yes, but whether it stayed that way for more than two weeks straight… I just can’t say for sure.\n\nMy memory goes off in pieces. I’ll remember the smell of the old bus station coffee, or what shoes I wore to a wedding forty years ago, but not the stretch of days well enough to answer that cleanly. So… I don’t want to guess and say yes or no. I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as intense, did you ever have a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a few ways, and I’m sorry, I don’t mean to be difficult. I just can’t honestly say.\n\nThere were times before this past year when I felt kind of down or not interested in much, but whether it was more than two weeks straight… I don’t have that clear in my head. I start trying to remember and then I’m thinking about the apartment I had back then, or the old radio in the kitchen, and I lose the thread of the days.\n\nSo the best I can tell you is: I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was shorter or not as strong, can you say for sure that you had a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t say for sure. That’s the honest answer.\n\nI know there were spells where I wasn’t myself, where things felt dull, but when you say “more than two weeks in a row,” I lose it. I start trying to count backward and then I’m remembering the curtains in my old place, or how the mail used to come late on Tuesdays, and it all gets mixed up.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you something definite when I don’t really know. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it wasn’t as severe or long-lasting, can you recall a specific time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row—like a clear stretch of time where you weren’t yourself?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a clear stretch like that, but I can’t pull one out for sure.\n\nI remember feeling off at different times, sure… but when I try to pin it down to “more than two weeks in a row,” it gets slippery. Several days, maybe, but I can’t put my finger on it cleanly.\n\nWhat I can tell you is now it’s very clear. I used to look forward to bridge, and fussing with the garden, and helping with the cooking at church. It’s like the wanting part is gone.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as strong, can you say for sure that there was a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Before this past year… I still can’t say for sure, no. Now I just sit in my chair and think about all that, and I can’t make myself care enough to do any of it. It’s like it’s all behind glass or something.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, even if it was not as severe, can you recall a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for a clear stretch of more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking about before this past year, but I still can’t honestly say yes to that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I don’t think I can recall it clearly, no. Before this past year, I had low spells, sure, but whether it was more than two weeks in a row… I just can’t pin that down.

Time gets funny when I try to look back. I start thinking about what season it was, whether the garden was doing well, or if we’d had tomatoes that year—my wife used to say I planted too many, but then everyone wanted some. And then I’ve lost the thread of what you asked.

So, no… I can’t say there was a definite stretch like that before this past year.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose what brings me in is I’ve been having these thoughts, and they don’t let up. I keep thinking my wife and the kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I know that’s a terrible thing to say.\n\nAnd lately I’ve been telling people—or maybe just muttering it, I don’t know—that I don’t need to see another winter. Winters used to be something, you know, when the kids were small and we’d shovel the walk, make cocoa… but now I just think, what’s the point of another one?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like making cocoa or being with your family?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I suppose that’s right. I used to look forward to things—bridge night, fussing over the garden, helping with the cooking at church. And I used to like going to Cubs games, even when they lost, which was often enough, but that was part of it somehow.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People say, “Come on, you’ll feel better if you go,” but I can’t seem to make myself care. It’s like all those things belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have you had times like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt so down and lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… oh, about a year now. A year and a month, maybe. It started after my bowling partner died. Funny, we weren’t even very good bowlers—he had this way of blaming the lanes every Tuesday—but after he was gone, things just… went flat. Since then, I don’t think I’ve really come back from it.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, everybody has spells, don’t they? After my mother passed, years ago, I was low for a bit, and when I retired it took some getting used to… I used to have a whole routine, coffee in the same mug, paper folded just so before work.\n\nBut more than two weeks like this? I don’t know. I can’t really say. It all blurs a little when I try to look back.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I… I really can’t say for sure. I know I had sad stretches, like anyone does—life sort of knocks you around, and then you get up and do the shopping or mow the lawn because it needs doing.\n\nBut more than two weeks in a row, and losing interest the way you mean… I don’t know. I’d be guessing. Funny, I can remember exactly where I kept my bowling shoes, but when you ask about how long I felt a certain way years back, it all gets muddy.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking the same thing, and I’m trying to pin it down for you, I really am. Before this last year… I just don’t know. There were bad patches, sure, but whether they were more than two weeks straight, or whether I lost interest like that… I can’t honestly say.\n\nIt’s strange, I can tell you the name of the man who ran the shoe counter at the bowling alley years ago, but this kind of thing just slips around when I try to remember it.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a clean answer for you, doctor. I really do. Before this past year… I remember feeling low here and there, but I can’t lay it out on a calendar and say, yes, that was two weeks straight.\n\nIt’s like when you open an old drawer and find receipts but not the one you need. I can remember little things—where I parked at the grocery store, or how my neighbor’s dog used to bark every morning—but whether I was down that long, I just don’t know for certain.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row, even if it wasn’t as severe or as long as this past year?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re saying—“even if it wasn’t this bad.” I just… I still can’t put my finger on it.\n\nBefore this past year, I had times where I was down, sure, but I don’t know if it ran more than two weeks in a row. Time gets funny looking back. I’ll remember some odd little thing, like the linoleum in our old kitchen or the way my brother used to tap his pencil, but not whether I felt that way for ten days or three weeks.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was a shorter time or not as intense, can you recall if you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to help me narrow it down, doctor, and I keep circling the same answer. I can remember being low before, yes, but whether it stayed that way for more than two weeks straight… I just can’t say for sure.\n\nMy memory goes off in pieces. I’ll remember the smell of the old bus station coffee, or what shoes I wore to a wedding forty years ago, but not the stretch of days well enough to answer that cleanly. So… I don’t want to guess and say yes or no. I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as intense, did you ever have a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a few ways, and I’m sorry, I don’t mean to be difficult. I just can’t honestly say.\n\nThere were times before this past year when I felt kind of down or not interested in much, but whether it was more than two weeks straight… I don’t have that clear in my head. I start trying to remember and then I’m thinking about the apartment I had back then, or the old radio in the kitchen, and I lose the thread of the days.\n\nSo the best I can tell you is: I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was shorter or not as strong, can you say for sure that you had a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t say for sure. That’s the honest answer.\n\nI know there were spells where I wasn’t myself, where things felt dull, but when you say “more than two weeks in a row,” I lose it. I start trying to count backward and then I’m remembering the curtains in my old place, or how the mail used to come late on Tuesdays, and it all gets mixed up.\n\nSo I don’t want to tell you something definite when I don’t really know. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it wasn’t as severe or long-lasting, can you recall a specific time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row—like a clear stretch of time where you weren’t yourself?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a clear stretch like that, but I can’t pull one out for sure.\n\nI remember feeling off at different times, sure… but when I try to pin it down to “more than two weeks in a row,” it gets slippery. I start thinking about where I was living then, and there was this little grocery store on the corner with a bell on the door—funny the things that stick—but the actual days, whether it was two weeks or just a few bad days spread out… I don’t know.\n\nSo no, I can’t recall a specific clear time before this past year. Not one I’d swear to.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was not as severe, can you recall a time when you felt down or stopped caring for a clear stretch of time—like several days in a row—where it felt like you were not yourself?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m still not sure about before this past year. I just can’t honestly say.\n\nThere were times before this past year when I felt kind of down or not interested in much, but whether it was more than two weeks straight… I don’t have that clear in my head. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it wasn’t as severe or long-lasting, can you recall a specific time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row—like a clear stretch of time where you weren’t yourself?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a clear stretch like that, but I can’t pull one out for sure.\n\nI remember feeling off at different times, sure… but when I try to pin it down to “more than two weeks in a row,” it gets slippery. I start thinking about where I was living then, and there was this little grocery store on the corner with a bell on the door—funny the things that stick—but the actual days, whether it was two weeks or just a few bad days spread out… I don’t know.\n\nSo no, I can’t recall a specific clear time before this past year. Not one I’d swear to.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was not as severe, can you recall a time when you felt down or stopped caring for a clear stretch of time—like several days in a row—where it felt like you were not yourself?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m still not sure about before this past year. Several days, maybe, but I can’t put my finger on it cleanly.\n\nWhat I can tell you is now it’s very clear. I used to look forward to bridge, and fussing with the garden, and helping with the cooking at church. I used to enjoy going to Cubs games too—though parking was always a mess, and I remember one time we sat way up where the wind just cut through you…\n\nBut now I mostly just sit in my chair. I think about those things and I can’t make myself care enough to do them. It’s like the wanting part is gone.\n\nDoctor: Before the past year, even if it was brief or not as strong, can you say for sure that there was a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Before this past year… I still can’t say for sure, no. I don’t want to make it sound like I remember a clean two-week stretch when I don’t.\n\nWhat I do know is how it is now. I used to look forward to bridge, and the garden, and helping with the cooking at church. And Cubs games—I used to get excited about those, even if the seats were uncomfortable and the hot dogs cost too much. Now I just sit in my chair and think about all that, and I can’t make myself care enough to do any of it. It’s like it’s all behind glass or something.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, even if it was not as severe, can you recall a time when you felt down or stopped caring about things for a clear stretch of more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking about before this past year, but I still can’t honestly say yes to that.
